# Ideas about a programming language, L, in time order
# From record/ideas/timeline.jsonl (sha256 b18b5806955d1b1377134fc1a570669b31d023e055b3f2c5d524ab5285f7615c), by naive_principles.idea_lines (naive_principles v1),

# Each line: r<n> | date | who | the idea

r1 | 2022-06-15 | the designer | With tapp he wanted to explore the idea that classes imply predicates.
r2 | 2022-06-15 | the designer | The idea that code is data; he notes it offers great flexibility and wonders why modern languages don't follow that Lispean path, and whether he should program macror that way.
r3 | 2022-06-15 | the designer | With quimulun the designer wanted to explore the importance of identifiers in programming.
r4 | 2022-06-15 | the designer | Re-making the language many times, each time rethinking the foundations of programming more deeply (quimulun, then tapp, now macror).
r5 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Defining a type channel state isomorphic with the type ONE, and a physical channel type also isomorphic with ONE.
r6 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Multiplicity of channels is expressed as a product, not a sum, an exponential to a number, in contrast with multiplicity of states as sum.
r7 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Second objective: once semantic validity and semantic equivalence are established, one can choose the most efficient path.
r8 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | The reason for coding is understanding, and understanding requires being able to manipulate the code and see how changes in it affect observables of the code.
r9 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Dependent types are types that depend on a value, which makes their cardinality that of the type of the value they take; the definition trails off unfinished.
r10 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | An equivalence relation between (state*k)^N and the sum over multi-indices of multinomial coefficients times state^a.
r11 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Establishing equivalence relations in quimulun matters because it allows optimizing computation times.
r12 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Creating new "objects" (values or variables, of types, functions or "classes") while losing the distinction between code and data.
r13 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Expressiveness includes expressing extra-computational concepts (e.g. probability) intuitively and truthfully, by abstracting their computational properties and providing mechanisms to verify compliance, e.g. a probability must be between 0 and 1, rejecting out-of-range values and reporting invalid results.
r14 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Code is more expressive when it refers to known structures or algorithms and the reference is real, e.g. not calling a list an array.
r15 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Expressiveness is an observable of code: the code must express its intention for both the human reader and the compiler.
r16 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | The problem of how to create values and their types, variables and types of variables, functions and types of functions, with the type of a variable marked uncertain.
r17 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | A library that lets one express variables and types, including functional and class types, with their semantics.
r18 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | First objective: abstract and formalize the mechanisms of semantic determination so there is a one-to-one relation between reasoning and the flow of the code, thereby verifying the validity of the reasoning; an old idea.
r19 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | A set of primitive functions: arithmetic, integer division, modulo, string concatenation, logical and comparison operators.
r20 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | A proposed set of primitive value types: boolean, integer, real, string, nothing (or zero), one (or something), memory, size, the last two marked uncertain.
r21 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Sample and probability form a duality, with tests that demonstrate it holds.
r22 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | This semantics lives in its own universe, not that of C++, even though it uses C++ structures to express it.
r23 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | A clear but inefficient algorithm can be optimized by a more cryptic one declared equivalent to it; the slow clear algorithm is easier to be convinced correct and serves as gold standard for the fast obscure one.
r24 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | Multiplicity of states is building "the channel is in one of n states" by multiplying the proposition "the channel is in a state" by a number of possibilities; the state i among N similar states has type estado x N, with N the type 1+1+...+1.
r25 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | A type constructor that takes a natural number and returns a set containing that number.
r26 | 2022-06-28 | the designer | An open question about how far to go with type theory and how far to use category theory.
r27 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | The environment has two fundamental mechanisms: storing new variables and retrieving old ones.
r28 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | The environment has two resources: memory to store variables and computational resources to manage that memory and store or retrieve variables.
r29 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | The environment is nothing but a map of definitions or values, a global variable at least where it is defined; the name for its entries (variables, constants) is still unsettled.
r30 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | The environment must also handle the execution of functions to generate new variables, which with more than one processor brings queue management, and supplying memory and compute to the functions.
r31 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | Loading data requires defining an environment first, so the environment is the first object the language has to define.
r32 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | There are simple environments (one type of value, a single identifier) and compound ones that admit multiple.
r33 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | Computation turns on identifying complex elements by a simple label; the environment provides this, returning a complex computational object from an identifying label, which lets programs stay short.
r34 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | The problem of compute and memory exceeds the environment yet is tied to it, since you need memory and compute to operate one; this is the task of the operating system and compiler.
r35 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | A variable is a name, a memory address, a memory size and a type; in fact the type contains the size and the name, if one wants, is the memory address.
r36 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | A variable knows its name and could know its environment, but better it does not, because if it did there would be no way to predict whether it will acquire further resources, especially since the environment can know its own environment.
r37 | 2022-07-05 | the designer | All variables descend from the environment, in that they can store and retrieve saved values as long as you give their name.
r38 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Code as data: a function's code is like a type's parameters, except that instead of numbers or types it holds operations; computation is running the functions.
r39 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Unlike Haskell where constructors live in the global space, perhaps they should live in each type's space (maybe.just vs just).
r40 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | The central idea of tapp: declaring a function means declaring what it must satisfy as a test, so automatic tests can be generated.
r41 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | A type must be universally accessible, like identifiers, so the definition of a type is necessarily recursive (temperature = type temperature ...).
r42 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | The difference between a constructor and a function: a function involves a computation (a further step) so no pattern matching; the constructor plus its parameters are the element, making elements transparent as if they had public members.
r43 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Two ways to define types, constructive (Haskell's data) and declarative (Haskell's class); a contrast with C++ concepts as predicates over types evaluated at compile time.
r44 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | A function holds its code written in tapp (script-like) plus some simplified compiled form.
r45 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Being able to apply functions that modify code: run tests, compute errors or derivatives, find equivalent codes, or estimate execution time.
r46 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | An inclination to follow the Lisp model, which would put a function's whole body into its parameters.
r47 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Names simplify programming; for dynamically created types or functions, a name is really an address or pointer, so a generated numeric identifier suffices.
r48 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | the designer wants the user-level process of defining new types and functions to be itself programmable, the language writing itself; this requires a simple, robust language with no syntactic sugar.
r49 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Each type (no distinction between type and function) should indicate the proposition it satisfies, in two versions, a computational one and a written one derived from it; propositions provable by static tests (from the algebra of propositions) or dynamic ones (checked element by element).
r50 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | By the symmetry between variable-value, variable-type and variable-function, the identifier and the type should be completely independent; the identifier is a fantasy name unknown until resolved.
r51 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Subtypes defined by a predicate over the value, as in ambient temperature constrained to a range.
r52 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | A wish to build tests, probabilities, errors and computation times into the language's syntax, together with equivalence relations for alternative functions, aiming at automatic self-optimization.
r53 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | In type theory an element relates to a type, but the problem is that one element satisfies many types at once.
r54 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | The chosen element-type relation: each element belongs to exactly one type but can serve to build many types, possibly via implicit conversion.
r55 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | A type or function can inject more than one identifier into the environment; being ultra-explicit about how names are injected requires some mechanism, e.g. variable binds [Maybe, Just, Nothing].
r56 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Composition is written with a dot ".", and this is said to explain class methods.
r57 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | The sign "=" is read as an assertion that one side equals the other; this is taken as a generating idea for tapp/quimulun.
r58 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | His proposal: use equals for assertions with predicates, to define subobject types, possibly indicated with guards "|".
r59 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | The equals sign is used for pattern matching when some symbol is unknown.
r60 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Identity is given with a universal law that id x equals x for all x.
r61 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | An io type carries write and read functions and laws that writing then reading recovers the value, and reading then writing recovers the string.
r62 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | The most important concept is the subObject: a subset of the algebraic object satisfying a predicate, a test.
r63 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | A test either succeeds or there is an explanation of why it fails.
r64 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Functions are of two types: predefined types and user defined.
r65 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Another criterion is to use "autodetection" of types.
r66 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | Types expressed as alternatives with "|", shown through maybe, either, and sum.
r67 | 2022-08-03 | the designer | How to express the type of a function write a : a->string is raised as an open question.
r68 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | From each run of the code something is learned: whether an implementation is correct, or by measuring its speed.
r69 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Code as data: being able to operate on function definitions, which requires distinguishing the code from its application.
r70 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Many functions must be defined together because their postconditions involve several functions at once, as in read and write or the operations of a mathematical object.
r71 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Two kinds of types: constructive ones (sum, product, exponential, function composition) and declarative ones that satisfy a proposition.
r72 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | The subtype defined by a function's postconditions is itself usable as a precondition of another function.
r73 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Functions are defined with preconditions (the subtypes of the arguments) and postconditions (propositions over arguments and returned values); the postconditions define the function's subtype.
r74 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Allow automatic optimization of the code as the resource consumption of alternative implementations of functions is measured.
r75 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | The language has parts: a tapp grammar definition with semantics rules, and a front compiler from tapp to C++.
r76 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | A wish to be able to define this kind of mathematical object.
r77 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Convert unsafe code into safe code, using types as encoders of preconditions.
r78 | 2023-02-22 | the designer | Convert untested code into tested code: an implementation belongs to a type only if it has been duly tested, and the testing conditions ensure the code's validity.
r79 | 2023-02-28 | the designer | A category declared as a class constructed from its components: objects, homs, composition and identity.
r80 | 2023-02-28 | the designer | Opens the question of how to declare a category in the language, and notes there could be different styles for doing so.
r81 | 2023-02-28 | the designer | Composition is a function on homs whose type carries the law that it is associative.
r82 | 2023-02-28 | the designer | One style for declaring a category modeled on C++, where there are classes or structs and functions.
r83 | 2023-02-28 | the designer | Id is a function giving an endo-hom whose type carries the left and right identity laws.
r84 | 2023-02-28 | the designer | Object is a type indexed by a category, and Hom is a type indexed by two objects.
r85 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Two notions kept from reading HoTT: declaration (saying something has a type) and definition (saying how to obtain the value).
r86 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Two kinds of definitions, the constructive and the declarative.
r87 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | The important question is how to express mathematical definitions (category, functor, group) in the language.
r88 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining functions of a given type (meeting certain postconditions) without stating the implementation.
r89 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | A functor is read as an implementation.
r90 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Whether to start from HoTT or from ZMT as the foundation.
r91 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining functions and giving an implementation that depends on functions whose implementation is unknown.
r92 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | A natural transformation is read as translating one implementation into another.
r93 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining variables of a given type without stating their value, i.e. parameters.
r94 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | The type is deduced by type calculation; if the derived type is larger than the parameter's type, this is flagged.
r95 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining function types by stating their postconditions.
r96 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining variable types by stating their postconditions.
r97 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining a tuple of variable and function types with postconditions involving the whole tuple (crossconditions).
r98 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | The conclusion that there is only one way to define, in tuples, which may hold one or more identifiers.
r99 | 2023-03-02 | the designer | Defining variables as functions of other variables or parameters, checking that the arguments meet the preconditions (belong to the predicted type).
r100 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | each assignment has an identifier to recover the created object, an equals sign, and an operation that creates the object.
r101 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | when the postconditions of objects are interdependent, those objects are assigned simultaneously.
r102 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | among the surveyed languages, Lisp's "code as data" is noted.
r103 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | a condition's result should not be just a boolean but either a one if met or a message explaining why it was not met.
r104 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | object creations verify that the pre- and postconditions of the created object are met.
r105 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | the most important structure in programming is the dictionary that joins human and machine, the basis of literalism.
r106 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | L is a chain of assignments.
r107 | 2023-03-13 | the designer | L is framed as the language for writing self-verified programs.

r248 | 2025-09-27 | the designer | A function should only be accepted into the compiler after it passes its test; the function keeps its pre/postcondition information even after being accepted, for continuous testing in debug mode.
r249 | 2025-09-27 | the designer | Each argument of each function gets a Domain; randomly selected sets of arguments can be sampled from it for a snapshot.

r253 | 2025-09-27 | model | FunctionContract is the full contract (domains, equivalence comparator, oracles, sampling/execution policy, side-effect policy, snapshot I/O, metadata), whereas a Postcondition is only "what must hold after a call."
r254 | 2025-09-27 | model | An Oracle supplies a reference behavior to compare against (expected output, acceptable set, bounds, differential or metamorphic relations), going beyond a Postcondition which checks a single result in isolation.
r255 | 2025-09-27 | the designer | Asks whether Oracle is just another name for postconditions on steroids.
r256 | 2025-09-27 | model | Postconditions are intrinsic predicates needing no external truth; oracles rely on an external reference or relation and can catch semantic drift the postcondition cannot when the exact expected value is unknown.
r257 | 2025-09-27 | the designer | In abstract terms, an oracle (like a postcondition) is still a function of arguments and output that evaluates to a Maybe_error<bool>.
r258 | 2025-09-27 | model | Both can be unified as a predicate over (Out, Args...) returning Maybe_error<bool>, but for oracles this is leaky: a tri-state (pass/fail/inconclusive), diagnostics, multi-run and policy hooks are needed, so a richer Verdict is recommended.

r288 | 2026-05-26 | the designer | El propósito final es que el usuario pueda encontrar distintas soluciones para su problema/ecuación y estimar la complejidad de cada forma de ejecución.
r289 | 2026-05-26 | the designer | The hardest problem is estimating the complexity of different implementations a priori; his answer is a Bayesian strategy: give chances to different algorithms doing the same thing and estimate the times they take for similar problems from the experience of actually running them. This answers the AI's question about what the initial language and the a-priori complexity evaluation would be.
r290 | 2026-05-26 | the designer | Ante el problema abierto de evaluar la complejidad a priori de distintos cómputos, L aplicaría una estrategia bayesiana: dar oportunidad a distintos algoritmos para la misma tarea, medir sus tiempos efectivos, y estimar a partir de la experiencia acumulada.

r538 | 2026-05-30 | the designer | Una idea fundamental de L es expresar distintos niveles de equivalencia — isomorfismo (contiene la misma información), igualdad exacta, misma ubicación en memoria, etc. — que dependen de algún tipo de functor de equivalencia diferente.

r571 | 2026-05-30 | the designer | Un programa L, estáticamente, se modela como un DAG (grafo dirigido acíclico). Esta es la estructura que sustenta la conmutatividad: el orden textual no importa porque la dependencia la da el grafo.
r572 | 2026-05-30 | the designer | Más allá del DAG estático de un programa, se puede tener un DAG que opera sobre DAGs, es decir, programas que manipulan programas. El DAG base es estático, pero hay algo que opera sobre él y lo transforma, usando instrucciones de sí mismo.

r616 | 2026-06-05 | the designer | La primera forma de la "función olvido": de un objeto que es la iteración de un algoritmo sobre una función en ciertas circunstancias, uno se olvida de todo el contexto y queda solo con una iteración. `get iteration` sobre el objeto devuelve una iteración de la que ya no se sabe de qué es.
r617 | 2026-06-05 | the designer | El olvido pensado como una proyección sobre uno de los ejes, en la que uno se olvida de los demás; una reducción de dimensionalidad.
r618 | 2026-06-05 | the designer | A first form of the "forget" function (`función olvido`): from an object that is the iteration of a given algorithm on a given function under given circumstances, `get iteration` drops everything and leaves a bare iteration; this forgetting is like a projection onto an axis, a reduction of dimensionality.
r619 | 2026-06-05 | the designer | The projections can be rejoined to recover the original object; the interesting case is projections that are disjoint or complementary, which would be "normal", though they may or may not be.
r620 | 2026-06-05 | the designer | Desde las proyecciones se puede reconstruir el objeto: uniendo todas las proyecciones se recupera el objeto original.

r663 | 2026-08-28 | the designer | What you gain by uploading your datum: the database gets your datum and you get to know where you stand relative to the rest; this knowing-where-you-are could be the minimal reciprocity that lets you win.

r665 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | El código tiene significado; pertenecer a un tipo equivale a afirmar una proposición. Es el origen conceptual de L.
r666 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Tener un código que representa los cálculos habilita autodiferenciación, tomar notas y obtener el resultado escrito paso por paso.
r667 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | If the code represents the calculations, you can autodifferentiate it, produce a step-by-step result, and get better error management; the point of L is that error management be better and that things be guaranteed.
r668 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Uno de los propósitos de L: mejor gestión del error y cosas garantizadas, porque tener una clase implica cumplir ciertas proposiciones.
r669 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | The core of L: code has meaning, and belonging to a type means asserting a proposition that the value satisfies.
r670 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Un valor o resultado de cálculo debería llevar algún tipo de anotación con todo lo que ya se sabe de su clase.
r671 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | A value carries an annotation of everything already known about it, and a forgetful functor drops some of it so the record does not grow to infinity; he says he reached the forgetful functor through L.
r672 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Mecanismo de ir olvidando información para que las anotaciones no crezcan al infinito. El autor dice haber llegado a este concepto a través de L ("al Funtor Olvido llegué por L" `[01:42]`).
r673 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Once a value is the result of a specific operation, it is that operation and not the generic one: the sum is the sum, not the magma.
r674 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Un valor sabe por qué operación concreta se produjo; si fue la suma, es la suma, y no la operación genérica de un magma.
r675 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | De una serie de operaciones se pueden tomar distintas vistas o niveles: el magma, el resultado, la ejecución, el código.
r676 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L como lenguaje inspirado en homotopy type theory, donde al definir una función queda ya definido el test sobre esa función.
r677 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | A language inspired by homotopy type theory in which defining a function already defines the test over it; you cannot define a function without its inverse and its test, and the test is the code itself. There are different levels of verification.
r678 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Leitmotiv de definir la likelihood y que eso genere un test; el valor puede estar sin testear o testeado, con distintos niveles de verificación.
r679 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | El tema de la velocidad del código como una de las preocupaciones de L.
r680 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L como anotador de código: se podría "luthierizar" código ajeno de Python (p. ej. tres o cuatro librerías de inversión de matriz), partiendo de la definición de qué es una inversión y qué propiedad debe cumplir.
r681 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L as an annotator of code: you could "luthierize" foreign code, e.g. several Python matrix-inversion libraries, starting from the definition of what an inversion is and the property it must satisfy, within a certain error.
r682 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Las propiedades (p. ej. que la inversa recupere el valor) se cumplen solo dentro de cierto error, porque muchas funciones inversas no son perfectas.
r683 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | A value is really an interval of values, or a sample of values (as in a Markov chain); the seno/arcoseno case shows how precision is kept or lost depending on whether the operation is remembered.
r684 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Un valor puede en realidad ser un intervalo de valores, o una sample de valores (por ejemplo una cadena de Markov representada por una sample).
r685 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | La precisión recuperable depende de cuánto de la historia operacional se conserva: si recordás que tomaste el seno de 4, el arcoseno devuelve 4 exacto; si olvidás la operación, queda un valor con intervalo y se pierde precisión. Liga el functor olvido a la precisión numérica.
r686 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Floating-point numbers do not follow the laws of numbers (associativity, etc.), but the code does; you assume the code follows them and reorganize it so execution is more efficient or more perfect.
r687 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Los floating points no cumplen las reglas algebraicas (asociatividad), pero el código sí se supone que las cumple, y por eso se lo puede reorganizar para que la ejecución sea más eficiente o más perfecta.
r688 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L should be a language that uses the AI itself, with the risk that it self-hacks.
r689 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L tendría que ser un lenguaje usado por la propia IA, no (solo) por un humano.
r690 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | la posibilidad de que el sistema/lenguaje se auto-hackee, señalada como algo a resolver.
r691 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Execution understood as a forgetful function: code that generates code, with two levels of execution, one that keeps the generated code and one that forgets it; you can keep the original function or regenerate the intermediate calculations.
r692 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Código que genera código: ejecutar es transcribir código o ejecutarlo, con dos niveles de ejecución, uno que guarda el código generado y otro que lo olvida. También lo formula como "la ejecución como una función olvido" `[09:09]`.
r693 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | Open question of what L is actually for: whether it would simplify macro IR, and the admission that so far it is an intellectual adventure whose objective he still has to find.

r695 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L como autoverificación: no se puede definir una función sin su inversa y su test, y el test es el propio código, de modo que correr el código corre también el test (a un determinado nivel, `[11:34]`).
r696 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L as a metalanguage, a specification with different implementations (PyLuthier, RLuthier, L CPP); the easiest start is a PyLuthier carrying the type as a class/attribute, though Python is not the right tool and the type ends up represented by fields and decorations rather than a true type.
r697 | 2026-08-31 | the designer | L no es una implementación sino un metalenguaje o conjunto de especificaciones, del que pueden existir varias implementaciones concretas (PyLuthier, un L en C++, etc.).

r704 | 2026-08-31 | model | Defining a function defines its test (likelihood with its simulation, a function with its inverse); running the code is running the test; foreign code can be "luthierized" and verified against the definition.
r705 | 2026-08-31 | model | The forget functor as central engineering problem: a decoration that records everything grows more than the value; need a simplifying type algebra and well-chosen forget functors, where what you forget defines the language.
r706 | 2026-08-31 | model | L is language-agnostic, a "butler" that orchestrates and annotates code in other languages; execution is delegated, what is L's own is description and verification of equivalences. Compared to Modelica.
r707 | 2026-08-31 | model | Path equivalence is not only proven logically but inferred statistically; L accumulates an empirical database of runs and generalizes bayesianly to predict future cost.
r708 | 2026-08-31 | model | L's code is an element of L from the start; a program is a DAG of definitions, statements commute, temporality emerges from causal dependency, nothing runs until defined.
r709 | 2026-08-31 | model | The model reports the designer's two new August motivations: a personal one (recovering contact with meaning now that AI writes all the code) and a social one (L as a platform where sharing correlated data makes you win).
r710 | 2026-08-31 | model | A function has many possible implementations (paths), each with its precision, compute time, memory, place of execution; L declares what to compute and compares equivalent paths estimating cost and error.
r711 | 2026-08-31 | model | The model's formulation of L's central idea as the designer stated it in his audios: types are propositions, belonging to a type asserts something guaranteed about the value.
r712 | 2026-08-31 | model | Defining a function defines its test (likelihood with its simulation, a function with its inverse), so running the code is running the test; one can "luthierize" foreign code by annotating it with the definition of what it should do and verifying it automatically.
r713 | 2026-08-31 | model | The founding quote chosen is Von Neumann's on making a machine do whatever one can state exactly.
r714 | 2026-08-31 | model | The forgetful functor as the central engineering problem: decoration grows faster than the value, so a simplifying type algebra and well-chosen forgetful functors are needed; what you forget defines the language, forgetting is a projection, and execution itself can be seen as a forget function, analyzed via the commutative square.
r715 | 2026-08-31 | model | The August personal motivation: since AI writes all the code and he no longer sees or feels it, L is the way to recover contact with meaning, having things guaranteed without looking at the code, and it should be a language the AI itself uses (with the doubt it might self-hack), done before access to the highly qualified "guide" is restricted.
r716 | 2026-08-31 | model | L is language-agnostic, a "mayordomo" that orchestrates and annotates code in other languages; execution is delegated, and what is proper to L is the description and verification of equivalences, compared to Modelica (specification without implementation).
r717 | 2026-08-31 | model | Open questions listed by the model: how to estimate complexity a priori, how to synthesize the run database without it being monstrous, which forgetful functors for what, whether HoTT models a whole program (contexts exist but are not operated on), and where to start the implementation (leaning toward a PyLuthier of decorators over Python, though Python is not the natural tool for real types).
r718 | 2026-08-31 | model | Equivalence between paths is not only proved logically but inferred statistically; L accumulates an empirical database of runs and generalizes with a Bayesian strategy, with HoTT giving the universe of points and particle filtering exploring the path space.

r751 | 2026-09-02 | the designer | Cómo se conectan y se expanden unos programas de L con otros, de modo que crezcan armónicamente; se lo propone como el problema fundamental.

r753 | 2026-09-02 | the designer | Compiling means checking whether the code contains errors, i.e. things incompatible with each other; many programs should not be compilable, because that is how errors get detected. This contrasts with algebraic calculation, where any expression computes something.
r754 | 2026-09-02 | the designer | Compilar es chequear si el código contiene errores (cosas incompatibles entre sí); a diferencia de un cálculo algebraico, se busca que muchos programas no sean compilables, porque así se detectan errores.
r755 | 2026-09-02 | the designer | Usar una librería implica requerir que siga ciertos patrones (funciones, definiciones) que cumplen ciertas proposiciones definidas desde L; ejemplo: un objeto matriz con inversión y las operaciones de álgebra lineal predefinidas.
r756 | 2026-09-02 | the designer | Libraries are handled by requiring that a library follow certain patterns, have certain functions and definitions that satisfy propositions defined from L; then an adapter fits that library to your particular use, so L works as a kind of universal adapter between different libraries.
r757 | 2026-09-02 | the designer | Mediante un adaptador que ajusta cada librería al uso particular, L serviría como adaptador universal entre distintas librerías.

r764 | 2026-09-13 | model | El objeto derivado (entradas, función, parámetros, resultado) es un término de prueba y su tipo es la proposición de que el resultado se obtuvo así; la base es un almacén de pruebas.
r765 | 2026-09-13 | model | L verifica postcondiciones muestreando entradas posibles; la verificación de postcondiciones y la calibración de cobertura son el mismo cómputo (el muestreador es `synth`).
r766 | 2026-09-13 | model | En L la precondición la garantiza el tipo del argumento, verificada al formar la referencia; las precondiciones que son predicados sobre valores piden tipos refinados.
r767 | 2026-09-13 | model | Un predicado sobre valores se expresa como tipo refinado, y una función que falla su postcondición produce un objeto de tipo "intento fallido" que igual se guarda.
r768 | 2026-09-13 | model | Cada función declara su tipo de resultado con campos fijos; el conjunto de tipos es abierto pero cada tipo es cerrado, una suma de tipos, no un blob JSON.
r769 | 2026-09-13 | the designer | Hay tipos "originales" generados por combinación de audios y cuestionarios; todos los demás son derivativos y por eso tienen estructura necesaria.
r770 | 2026-09-13 | the designer | Precisa que por derivativo entiende resultado de funciones.

r772 | 2026-09-13 | model | Las correcciones son afirmaciones humanas (originales), no ediciones; un objeto derivado nunca se edita, y así etiquetas, control de calidad y correcciones caen en el mismo lugar.

r782 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | What are the most fundamental elements of a program? He proposes candidates: elementary types, elementary functions, code vs data (as in R, which moves between them), true/false, true-by-definition, address/data (pointer and data).
r783 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | L should be executable on paper; it should be minimalist and recursive, taking the most expressive and natural from lambda calculus, Lisp, C++, Python, a language Knuth would like, with code as a first-class citizen.
r784 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | Propone seis candidatos a elemento más fundamental: tipos elementales, funciones elementales, código vs dato (como R), verdadero/falso, verdadero por definición, dirección y dato.
r785 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | Antes de definir tipos y funciones hay que definir los elementos del lenguaje; L debe poder ejecutarse en papel, ser minimalista y recursivo, con el código como ciudadano de primera.
r786 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | L toma de lambda calculus, Lisp, C++ y la naturalidad de Python, apuntando a un lenguaje que le gustaría a Knuth.
r787 | 2026-09-28 | model | Code vs data in the Lisp form, not R's: `Program` is an inductive type and `eval` a partial function `Program → Name → Maybe Term`; no `eval` injects arbitrary terms into the type system, which prevents self-hacking.
r788 | 2026-09-28 | model | The most fundamental element is not the type or the function but the definition, `nombre : Tipo := término`; a program is a finite unordered set of definitions and the DAG comes from which names each uses.
r789 | 2026-09-28 | model | Address/data is rejected: the name is the reference, the memory address is a decoration of the lowest equivalence level; without mutation and time a pointer has nothing to distinguish.
r790 | 2026-09-28 | model | Two definitions of the same name are not an error nor a cartesian product but two implementations, `f#1` and `f#2`; the bare name `f` is usable only when a path `f#1 = f#2` exists, putting paths in the grammar without new primitives.
r791 | 2026-09-28 | model | Elementary types and functions are not primitive; the primitives are the formers (`U`, `Π`, `data`). `N` is defined with `data`, addition by recursion, `R` is a structure with axioms, `seno` is a hole.
r792 | 2026-09-28 | model | The hole, a definition with type and no body `f : A → B := ?`, plays five roles at once (axiom, program input, external function, pending definition, dead code); "you don't execute until it's defined" is its exact semantics, removing the need for an `External` type.
r793 | 2026-09-28 | model | "True by definition" is the definitional equality `≡` (two terms reducing to the same normal form), a judgment the reducer decides, distinct from `=`, the identity type ("true with evidence") whose terms are paths; this makes "the test is the code itself" literal.
r794 | 2026-09-28 | model | The core is five pieces: the definition (with the hole), lambda calculus with reduction, `U`, `Π`, `data`; from `data` come `Σ`, `=`, `⊥`, `⊤`, `N`, `Program`; forgetting is `fst` of a `Σ`; Bayes, run database and numbers-with-error stay outside as libraries.
r795 | 2026-09-28 | model | What to attack next, in order: choose the recursor (without the dependent one there are no general paths proven by induction), then the concrete syntax, since only read aloud will it be known if it is a language Knuth would like.
r796 | 2026-09-28 | model | True/false is not `Bool`: a proposition is a type, true if it has a term; `⊤` and `⊥` are the extremes, `Bool` is a datum with two constructors.
r797 | 2026-09-28 | model | Código vs dato en la forma de Lisp y no de R: `Program` es un tipo inductivo y `eval : Program → Name → Maybe Term` es parcial; no hay `eval` que inyecte términos arbitrarios en el sistema de tipos, lo que impide el auto-hackeo.
r798 | 2026-09-28 | model | El nombre es la referencia; la dirección de memoria es una decoración del nivel más bajo de equivalencia. Sin mutación ni tiempo, un puntero no tiene qué distinguir.
r799 | 2026-09-28 | model | Dos definiciones del mismo nombre no son error ni producto cartesiano sino dos implementaciones `f#1`, `f#2`; el nombre pelado `f` solo se usa cuando existe un camino `f#1 = f#2`. Esto pone los paths en la gramática sin primitivas nuevas.
r800 | 2026-09-28 | model | El hueco, una definición con tipo y sin cuerpo, cumple cinco papeles a la vez (axioma, input, función externa, definición pendiente, código muerto), y el principio "no ejecutás hasta que está definido" es su semántica.
r801 | 2026-09-28 | model | La regla que une `≡` y `=` (si `a ≡ b` entonces `refl` tipa como `a = b`) hace literal que el test es el propio código: testear una instancia es reducir y ver que `refl` tipa; probar el `∀` es construir el camino.
r802 | 2026-09-28 | model | Lo primitivo son los formadores (`U`, `Π`, `data`), no `int` ni `seno`; `N` se define con `data`, la suma por recursión, `R` con axiomas, `seno` es un hueco.
r803 | 2026-09-28 | model | "Verdadero por definición" es la igualdad definicional `≡` (dos términos que reducen a la misma forma normal), un juicio que decide el reductor; se distingue de `=`, el tipo identidad, "verdadero con evidencia", cuyos términos son los paths.
r804 | 2026-09-28 | model | El elemento más fundamental no es el tipo ni la función sino la definición `nombre : Tipo := término`; un programa es un conjunto finito de definiciones sin orden, y el DAG sale de qué nombres usa cada una.
r805 | 2026-09-28 | model | El núcleo queda en cinco piezas (la definición con el hueco, el cálculo lambda con reducción, `U`, `Π`, `data`); con `data` se definen `Σ`, `=`, `⊥`, `⊤`, `N`, `Program`, y Bayes/base de corridas/números con error quedan fuera como librerías.
r806 | 2026-09-28 | model | El olvido es `fst` de un `Σ`; qué olvidar es política de librería.
r807 | 2026-09-28 | model | Lo único primitivo fuera del sistema es el pacto de arranque: la implementación acepta un valor de `Program` y lo trata como programa.
r808 | 2026-09-28 | model | Verdadero y falso no como `Bool`: una proposición es un tipo, verdadera si tiene un término; `⊤` y `⊥` son los extremos y `Bool` es un dato más con dos constructores.
r809 | 2026-09-28 | model | Lo primero a atacar después es elegir el recursor; sin el dependiente no se puede probar el `∀` por inducción, o sea no hay paths generales; luego la sintaxis concreta.
r810 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | the designer asks whether a modern take would use the same primitive elements or whether there are alternative ways to acquire Turing universality.
r811 | 2026-09-28 | the designer | the designer wants to implement his language L and is looking for its primitive atomic elements, drawing inspiration from Lisp, Haskell, C++, lambda calculus, Python and R, aiming at a universal semantics like Lisp's.
r812 | 2026-09-28 | model | The model proposes the compiler expose its AST as a data type and macros be ordinary functions Expr → Expr run at compile time, so all surface syntax desugars to the core.
r813 | 2026-09-28 | model | The model proposes the core include controlled mutation cells (alloc/load/store) and linear/affine ownership so functional transforms can mutate buffers in place without GC.
r814 | 2026-09-28 | model | The model proposes six operational primitives (var, lambda, apply, case/match, letrec, quote) to which any L program reduces, with the conditional subsumed by pattern matching on boolean constructors.
r815 | 2026-09-28 | model | The model proposes separating a minimal ontological core (what the evaluator understands) from the surface syntax and sugar.
r816 | 2026-09-28 | model | The model proposes a labeled immutable tuple/structure as the unifying atomic datum, unifying Lisp s-expressions and Haskell ADTs, with contiguous same-type vectors as a special case for performance.
r817 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Why do we need a symbol `?` for the hole? Isn't declaring `f : A -> B` and not defining it enough?
r818 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Cuestiona la necesidad del símbolo `?` para el hueco: basta declarar `f: A -> B` sin definirla.
r819 | 2026-09-29 | model | The symbol is not needed in the core; the primitive is "declaration without body": `f : A → B` alone is already a hole, `f` an opaque name, `f a` doesn't reduce. `?` is useful only inside a term and even then is derivable sugar by lifting to a declaration of `Π` type.
r820 | 2026-09-29 | model | The bare hole does not mark one distinction, that of intention not semantics: whether a bodyless function will be filled by numpy or is just unwritten; the core need not distinguish, `huecos : Program → List Name` lists all, intention goes in the definition's text field if adopted.
r821 | 2026-09-29 | model | El núcleo no necesita `?`: lo primitivo es "declaración sin cuerpo", `f : A → B` ya es un hueco, nombre opaco que no reduce; `?` es azúcar de superficie, derivable levantando el hueco a una declaración de tipo `Π` sobre las variables locales.
r822 | 2026-09-29 | model | Un hueco dentro de un término permite que el verificador acepte lo escrito y diga el tipo exacto de lo que falta (el "goal" de Agda); es "el compilador con buenos mensajes de qué está ocurriendo".
r823 | 2026-09-29 | model | El núcleo no distingue por qué un hueco está sin cuerpo (lo llena numpy o no se escribió aún); `huecos : Program → List Name` los lista a todos y la intención va en el campo de texto de la definición, si se adopta.
r824 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Thinking of a more functional version, less HoTT, more specific about types; L should be thought of as a language that indicates knowledge; asks what `a : A` means (A is true and a is a proof, or that there are cases where A is true, a among them).
r825 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Quiere pensar una versión más funcional, menos HoTT y más específica con los tipos, y pensar L como un lenguaje que indica conocimiento; pregunta si `a : A` significa que A es verdad y a es prueba, o que existen casos donde A es verdad.
r826 | 2026-09-29 | model | `a : A` is a judgment, not a proposition; what it means depends on the kind of type `A` is (mere proposition: "A holds, a is how I know"; set: "a is one of A's elements"). The "language that indicates knowledge" is exactly Martin-Löf's reading, where the hole is an epistemic assumption.
r827 | 2026-09-29 | model | "More specific with types" means types are data and knowledge about a value is carried as an evidence component in a `Σ`, marked erasable; `x : Σ (v : R). 0 < v` is a real plus what's known of it, same object as a refinement type, but the designer wants the evidence sometimes written and kept, so the erasable mark is set by who defines, not the language.
r828 | 2026-09-29 | model | The one distinction to keep from HoTT for the functional version: knowing *that* (a mere proposition's proof) vs knowing *which* (a set's value); the passage from the second to the first is the most basic forgetting, truncation, where the forget functor begins.
r829 | 2026-09-29 | model | `a : A` no es una proposición sino un juicio; su significado depende de qué clase de tipo es `A` (los niveles de HoTT): proposición mera ("A vale, y a es cómo lo sé") o conjunto ("a es uno de los elementos de A").
r830 | 2026-09-29 | model | Los tipos son datos y el conocimiento sobre un valor se lleva como componente de evidencia en un `Σ`, marcado como borrable (como un refinement type), pero en L la evidencia a veces se escribe y se guarda, y la marca de borrable la pone quien define, no el lenguaje.
r831 | 2026-09-29 | model | "Lenguaje que indica conocimiento" es la lectura de Martin-Löf: un juicio es un acto de conocimiento; `f : A → B := t` dice "sé pasar de A a B, y t es el método", y el hueco sin cuerpo es un supuesto epistémico, una hipótesis u oráculo.
r832 | 2026-09-29 | model | La distinción que deja de HoTT para la versión funcional es saber *que* (prueba de una proposición mera) contra saber *cuál* (valor de un conjunto); el pasaje del segundo al primero es la truncación, donde empieza el functor olvido.
r833 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | In `Σ (x : X). A x`, what does the dot mean?
r834 | 2026-09-29 | model | The dot is a notational separator marking where the bound variable ends and the body begins, the same dot as in `λ x. t`; worth deciding one convention for the three binding forms (`λ`, `Π`, `Σ`), an open concrete-syntax decision.
r835 | 2026-09-29 | model | El punto en `Σ (x : X). A x` es un separador de notación entre la variable ligada y el cuerpo, el mismo que en `λ x. t` y `Π (x : A). B`; para L conviene una sola convención para las tres formas ligadoras.
r836 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Suggests the dot is like `{` in `f(x) { return x*x }`.
r837 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Proposes reducing L's syntax to only declarations and instantiations, and asks for a critical analysis of two concrete notations for defining a function.
r838 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Reducir la sintaxis de L a solo declaraciones e instanciaciones; pide análisis crítico entre dos notaciones de función.
r839 | 2026-09-29 | model | A parameterless lambda should not exist in L: without time or effects, `\ () { a + a }` and `a + a` are the same value, a consequence of the commutativity of statements.
r840 | 2026-09-29 | model | Recommends the second notation `f : A -> A := \ (x) { x + x }` separating type from term: instantiation is declaration plus `:= término`, one way to build functions (lambda) and one to write their type (arrow), serving named and anonymous alike; the first notation (C/Rust header) duplicates the signature and isn't minimal.
r841 | 2026-09-29 | model | One binding form `nombre : Tipo` appearing identically in declaration, parameter, dependent type and pair, with `A -> B` sugar for `(_ : A) -> B` and the dot disappearing; braces delimit a block that can hold local definitions, giving local definitions without new primitives (a block is a `Program` with an output-marked definition).
r842 | 2026-09-29 | model | Las llaves delimitan un bloque que puede contener definiciones locales; un bloque es un `Program` con una definición marcada como salida, dando definiciones locales sin primitivas nuevas.
r843 | 2026-09-29 | model | Una lambda sin parámetros no debería existir en L: sin tiempo ni efectos, `\ () { a + a }` y `a + a` son el mismo valor; es consecuencia de la conmutatividad de statements.
r844 | 2026-09-29 | model | Recomienda la notación `f : A -> A := \ (x) { x + x }` que separa el tipo del término sobre la que junta tres cosas en la cabecera; una sola forma de construir funciones (la lambda) y una de escribir su tipo (la flecha), y el cuerpo es un término, lo que hace al código dato.
r845 | 2026-09-29 | model | Una sola forma para ligar nombres, `nombre : Tipo`, igual en la declaración, el parámetro, el tipo dependiente y el par; `A -> B` es azúcar de `(_ : A) -> B` y el alcance lo dan paréntesis y llaves.
r846 | 2026-09-29 | model | Usar `:=` para definir, no `=`, porque `=` se usará como proposición (tipo identidad) en las fórmulas, y `f : A -> A = t` sería ambiguo.
r847 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Confirms braces are fundamental, and asks whether to use `:=` and `=` or `=` and `==` for definition vs equality.
r848 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Confirma que las llaves son fundamentales, y pregunta si usar `:=` y `=` o `=` y `==`.
r849 | 2026-09-29 | model | Use `:=` to define and `=` for the identity type (the proposition), reserving `==` for decidable equality (library); `≡`, the definitional one, is not written in programs. Argument: in math `=` is the symmetric proposition, `==` reads as a runtime boolean test, and definition is asymmetric. This is Lean's convention.
r850 | 2026-09-29 | model | Tres igualdades, cada una con su símbolo: `:=` define (acto asimétrico), `=` es el tipo identidad (proposición), `==` la igualdad decidible de librería `A -> A -> Bool`, y `≡` la definicional que no se escribe en programas. Es la convención de Lean.
r851 | 2026-09-29 | model | `≡` is not written in the program but constated by the verifier (or by hand); to assert a definitional equality as a check within a program the way is `_ : a = b := refl`, which types only if `a ≡ b`, so no fourth symbol is needed.
r852 | 2026-09-29 | model | `:=` is the act (giving a name a body) while `≡` is the relation that follows from all the `:=` plus the computation rules (lambda substitution, recursor rules); each `:=` gives a `≡` but `≡` is larger. "True by definition" in the broad sense is true by unfolding and computing.
r853 | 2026-09-29 | model | `:=` es el acto que da cuerpo a un nombre; `≡` es la relación consecuencia de todos los `:=` más las reglas de cómputo (sustitución de la lambda, recursor), más grande que las definiciones. "Verdadero por definición" en sentido amplio es verdadero por desplegar y computar.
r854 | 2026-09-29 | model | Para afirmar en un programa que dos términos son definicionalmente iguales como chequeo, el modo es `_ : a = b := refl`, que tipa solo si `a ≡ b`; así no hace falta un cuarto símbolo.
r855 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Pregunta qué significa "tipa".
r856 | 2026-09-29 | model | Application binds tighter than any operator, so `f a + g b` is `(f a) + (g b)`, the convention that makes formulas read as formulas.
r857 | 2026-09-29 | model | Use juxtaposition `f a` in the core: `f(a)` is the same since `(a)` is `a` in parens, so it forces no choice; `f a b` is curried application, `f (a, b)` applies a function to a pair, the two coexisting without ambiguity as distinct types; multiplication must be explicit `2 * x`.
r858 | 2026-09-29 | model | Juxtaposición `f a` en el núcleo, porque `f(a)` es lo mismo (paréntesis sobre `a`); `f a b` asocia a la izquierda con aplicación parcial gratis, y `f (a, b)` aplica a un par, dos funciones de tipos distintos que conviven sin ambigüedad. La aplicación liga más fuerte que cualquier operador.
r859 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | `f a` is three things that are all used: `f a` unevaluated, `a + a` evaluated, and `f a -> a + a` (both).
r860 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | `f a` es tres cosas que se usan: `f a` sin evaluar, `a + a` evaluado, y `f a -> a + a` (la corrida con ambos).
r861 | 2026-09-29 | model | What remains to decide is how to write `f a` as data within a program without writing `app (ref f) (ref a)` by hand; the Lisp quote `'(f a)` as sugar, the parser producing the `Term` constructors.
r862 | 2026-09-29 | model | Since `≡` is confluent, the order of steps affects only cost and termination, not the result; this is the designer's "distintos paths con distinta velocidad" at the finest grain, two traces being two paths between the same points. "Unevaluated" is a syntactic datum, not a runtime state like a thunk, since there is no time.
r863 | 2026-09-29 | model | Inside a program `f a` and `a + a` are `≡` (referential transparency); the distinction lives only when the program looks at its own code, as three data: the unevaluated term (code), the normal form (result), and the run `f a -> a + a` as a term of an inductive type `Traza t u`. `eval` has two versions, one that remembers and one that forgets the run.
r864 | 2026-09-29 | model | Para escribir `f a` como dato dentro de un programa sin escribir `app (ref f) (ref a)` a mano, se usa la comilla de Lisp `'(f a)`, azúcar que el parser convierte en constructores de `Term`.
r865 | 2026-09-29 | model | Como `≡` es confluente, el orden de los pasos no afecta el resultado, solo el costo y si termina; dos trazas distintas de `f a` a `a + a` son dos caminos entre los mismos puntos, el grano más fino de "distintos paths con distinta velocidad".
r866 | 2026-09-29 | model | `eval` tiene dos versiones, los dos niveles de ejecución: `eval_r` devuelve resultado y corrida, `eval_o` olvida la corrida con `fst`; el orden de olvido es corrida, luego resultado con la proposición, luego solo resultado.
r867 | 2026-09-29 | model | "Sin evaluar" no es un estado en tiempo de ejecución (un thunk) porque no hay tiempo; es un dato sintáctico, lo que elimina la semántica de evaluación perezosa.
r868 | 2026-09-29 | model | Dentro de un programa `f a` y `a + a` son `≡` (transparencia referencial); la distinción aparece al mirar el propio código en `Term`/`Program`, donde las tres cosas son tres datos: el término sin evaluar (código), la forma normal (resultado relativo a los huecos), y la corrida como `Traza t u`, un tipo inductivo con un constructor por regla.
r869 | 2026-09-29 | model | `Term` as defined is untyped syntax (`app (con "zero") univ` is valid though senseless); whether it has sense is decided by `check`. A `Term` admitting only well-typed terms exists and is more elegant but much harder; raw syntax plus `check` suffices to start, as almost all real verifiers do.
r870 | 2026-09-29 | model | `Term` is the type of L's syntax defined in L itself with `data`, one constructor per form of writing a term; code is the first element not by a magic operator but because syntax is a `data` like `N` or `List`. It is untyped syntax, sense decided by `check`, and is metacircular, leaving only the bootstrap outside.
r871 | 2026-09-29 | model | `Term` es un tipo de L, así que su definición es un `Def` de `Program`: el verificador escrito en L puede verificar el programa que lo define; la única pieza afuera es el arranque en C que lea un `Program` y corra `eval` por primera vez.
r872 | 2026-09-29 | model | `Term` así es sintaxis sin tipar; que tenga sentido lo decide `check`, donde vive "el programa no compila". La alternativa de un `Term` solo bien tipado es más elegante pero mucho más difícil; para empezar, sintaxis cruda más `check` alcanza.
r873 | 2026-09-29 | model | `Term` es el tipo de la sintaxis de L, definido en L mismo con `data`, un constructor por forma de escribir un término; el código es el primer elemento porque la sintaxis es un `data` como `N` o `List`, y `eval`/`check`/`huecos` son funciones ordinarias sobre `Program`.
r874 | 2026-09-29 | model | The only non-L thing is a bootstrap program (in C or anything) that reads the text, parses it into a `Program`, and mechanically applies `recTerm` and substitution until reaching `eval`; once `eval` written in L runs on itself, the bootstrapper can be thrown away. That is the concrete self-extensibility.
r875 | 2026-09-29 | model | Substitution has a known capture problem; the cheap fix for the by-hand version is requiring all bound names distinct, the serious fix is de Bruijn indices (changing `ref : Name -> Term` to `var : N -> Term` for bound variables), left for later but to be known.
r876 | 2026-09-29 | model | `eval` repeating `paso` is not structural recursion and does not always terminate, so the verifier cannot accept it; the honest exit is fuel, `eval : N -> Program -> Term -> Maybe Term`, making it total, and "how much fuel was needed" is the first cost measure, for free.
r877 | 2026-09-29 | model | `data` gives three things: the type, the constructors (ordinary bodyless functions in normal form), and the recursor (one case per constructor, with a reduction rule each); every function over the type is an instance of this recursor, and that is all the core provides, the rest being definitions.
r878 | 2026-09-29 | model | Todo lo escrito son definiciones de L; lo que no es L es un programa en C que lee el texto, lo convierte en `Program` y aplica las reglas hasta `eval`; una vez que `eval` corre sobre sí mismo el arrancador se puede tirar. Esa es la autoextensibilidad concreta.
r879 | 2026-09-29 | model | La sustitución tiene el problema de captura de variables; la solución barata a mano es exigir nombres ligados todos distintos, la seria es índices de de Bruijn, que cambia `ref : Name -> Term` por `var : N -> Term` para las ligadas.
r880 | 2026-09-29 | model | `check` recorre las definiciones y verifica que cada tipo sea un tipo y que el cuerpo tenga el tipo declarado; `infer` calcula el tipo por recursión sobre `Term`, y los verificadores reales son bidireccionales (unas formas infieren, otras se chequean contra un tipo). Ahí es donde `refl` tipa o no.
r881 | 2026-09-29 | model | `eval` por repetir `paso` no es recursión estructural y no siempre termina; la salida honesta es darle combustible (`eval : N -> Program -> Term -> Maybe Term`), con lo que es total y "cuánto combustible hizo falta" es la primera medida de costo gratis.
r882 | 2026-09-29 | model | Un paso de reducción tiene tres reglas: beta (sustituir una lambda), delta (desplegar una definición), iota (correr el recursor); el orden de las cláusulas es la estrategia de evaluación, y otra elección da otra traza para el mismo resultado.
r883 | 2026-09-29 | model | A `data` is a sum of products plus recursion: sum between constructors (disjoint union), product inside each constructor, and recursion (the type inside its own definition, the least fixpoint). `+` and `×` are the degenerate cases, so `data` is primitive and they are not; indexed families (like `Vec`, `Traza t u`) are a fourth layer.
r884 | 2026-09-29 | model | Una cuarta capa de `data` son constructores cuyo tipo de retorno lleva un índice (`Vec : N -> U`), suma entre constructores de tipos distintos de una misma familia; es lo que permite que `Traza t u` tenga constructores solo para pares que se reducen uno al otro.
r885 | 2026-09-29 | model | Un `data` es suma de productos más recursión: suma entre constructores, producto dentro de cada uno, y recursión porque el tipo aparece en su propia definición (el menor punto fijo). `+` y `×` son los casos degenerados, por eso `data` es primitivo.
r886 | 2026-09-29 | model | Falta un octavo constructor de `Term`: la representación del propio `data { ... }` como término, para que `N : U := data {...}` sea representable.
r887 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Wants to define invariants: `f : A -> B` and for all `x : A`, `g x (f x)` where `g : A -> B -> bool`.
r888 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Quiere poder definir invariantes: `f : A -> B` y para todo `x : A`, `g x (f x)` con `g : A -> B -> bool`.
r889 | 2026-09-29 | model | "For all `x : A`" is `(x : A) ->`, the `Π`; the only thing to resolve is lifting `g`'s `Bool` to a proposition, cleanly done with a `data` `Cierto : Bool -> U` that is `⊤` when true and `⊥` when false, so the evidence for a concrete `x` is `unit` and types exactly when the boolean reduces to `true`.
r890 | 2026-09-29 | model | Two ways to tie the invariant to `f`: as a separate definition (`f_inv` a hole until proven, the declaration being the test and filling it the proof, "defining the function defines the test"), or as a package `F := (h : A -> B) × (...)` where the guarantee travels with the function, the "function defined operationally and by its properties" of the May 29 audio.
r891 | 2026-09-29 | model | The verification levels are the same declaration with different degrees of body: running `test_f` on a sample is empirical verification (each passing instance a `unit` that types by computation), filling `casos` for all `x` is formal verification, and in between goes the statistical extension (a sample of passed instances with its size as evidence of a `≈` type to be defined later).
r892 | 2026-09-29 | model | "Para todo" es el `Π`; un invariante tiene que ser una proposición (un tipo), y hay dos puentes desde `Bool`: `g x (f x) = true`, o un `data` `Cierto` que es `⊤` cuando es `true` y `⊥` cuando es `false`. La tercera forma, más general, es escribir la propiedad como tipo `G : A -> B -> U` sin pasar por `Bool`.
r893 | 2026-09-29 | model | Atar el invariante a `f` de dos formas: como definición separada `f_inv` (un hueco mientras no se prueba, "definir la función define el test"), o como paquete `F := (h : A -> B) × ((x) -> Cierto (g x (h x)))` donde la garantía viaja con la función (la del audio del 29 de mayo).
r894 | 2026-09-29 | model | Los tres niveles de verificación son la misma declaración con distinto grado de cuerpo: correr `test_f` sobre una muestra (empírica), llenar `casos` para todo `x` (formal), y en el medio la extensión estadística, una muestra con su tamaño como evidencia de un tipo `≈` a definir.
r895 | 2026-09-29 | model | Proving `ley1` in general by induction requires the result type to depend on `y` (one thing for `zero`, another for `succ y'`), which is the dependent recursor (induction); the simple recursor cannot write it, so `data` gives the dependent recursor.
r896 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | What is needed is a form that is expressive and not too redundant.
r897 | 2026-09-29 | model | Laws are declarations like any other, holes until proven, and falsifiable by an instance that does not type; fixing `ley2` can go either as a hypothesis in the type (`Cierto (leq y x) ->`) with `sub` total and guarantee partial, or as a restriction in the function (`sub'`) with function partial and guarantee total, the same information in a different place.
r898 | 2026-09-29 | model | "Subtraction inverse of addition" is two laws; over `N` one holds (`ley1`) and the other (`ley2`) is false as declared, discovered by a one-line test that does not type (`t2`). The type system tells you before you find out the hard way; this is "express to discard", a law being a hole no one can ever fill.
r899 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Lo que se necesita es una forma expresiva y no demasiado redundante.
r900 | 2026-09-29 | model | `ley2` se arregla de dos formas con la misma información en otro lugar: `sub` total con `ley2` condicionada por `Cierto (leq y x)`, o `sub'` parcial que solo se llama con evidencia y cuya ley vale sin condiciones; cuál conviene depende de quién usa la resta.
r901 | 2026-09-29 | model | Las leyes son declaraciones como cualquier otra, huecos hasta que se prueban y falsables por una instancia que no tipa; el ejemplo fijó que el recursor es dependiente y que hacen falta `refl`, `cong`, `absurdo` de librería sobre `=` y `⊥`.
r902 | 2026-09-29 | model | Probar `ley1` por inducción exige que el tipo del resultado dependa de `y`, el recursor dependiente; con el recursor simple no se puede escribir, así que queda decidido que `data` da el dependiente.
r903 | 2026-09-29 | model | "Resta inversa de suma" son dos leyes y sobre `N` una vale (`ley1`) y la otra no (`ley2`); `ley2` es falsa tal como fue declarada y se descubre con un test de una línea que no tipa. Esto es "expresar para descartar".
r904 | 2026-09-29 | model | Proposes a criterion for any future sugar: if it forces adding a constructor to `Term` it is not sugar and must be discussed as a primitive.
r905 | 2026-09-29 | model | There are two kinds of redundancy; the one that crosses the `:=` (type says one thing, body another, verifier checks they match) is what gives something to verify; the one repeated on the same side is removable with sugar.
r906 | 2026-09-29 | model | Clauses written against a header mean the form of a definition can depend on reducing its type; the model accepts it as the same `≡` at work.
r907 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Rejects doing propositional logic; the aim is to generate numeric tests.
r908 | 2026-09-29 | model | Propone un criterio para toda azúcar futura: si obliga a agregar un constructor a `Term`, no es azúcar sino primitiva y hay que discutirla aparte.
r909 | 2026-09-29 | model | Acepta que la forma de una definición (cuántos argumentos tienen sus cláusulas) pueda depender de reducir su tipo; es el mismo `≡` trabajando.
r910 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Rechaza la capa de demostración (lógica proposicional) y fija el objetivo de la versión como generar tests numéricos.
r911 | 2026-09-29 | model | Distingue dos clases de redundancia: la que cruza el `:=` (tipo contra cuerpo, lo que el verificador comprueba) es la que vale; la que se repite del mismo lado se elimina con azúcar sin tocar el núcleo.
r912 | 2026-09-29 | model | For numeric tests the proof layer (`=`, `refl`, induction) can not exist in version 0; an invariant is just a function returning `Bool`, plus an input generator, running and storing the result; "defining the function defines its test" is a field in `Def`.
r913 | 2026-09-29 | model | Las leyes de una función son una lista de funciones a `Bool` atada al nombre, un campo más de `Def` al lado del cuerpo; el generador es un hueco que el entorno llena por tipo; la corrida es un dato.
r914 | 2026-09-29 | model | La versión 0 no necesita `=`, `refl`, inducción ni recursor dependiente; un invariante es una función que devuelve `Bool`, con un generador de entradas por tipo y una corrida que guarda el resultado; el contraejemplo lo encuentra la muestra.
r915 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Pide que las leyes se expresen en pocas líneas, inspirándose en cómo se expresan los conceptos en C++ con `requires`.
r916 | 2026-09-29 | model | Borrow the C++ `requires` form: a block with quantified variables in the header and one `Bool` expression per line; a type requirement is checked by the verifier without running, a value requirement is run by the test, both in the same block.
r917 | 2026-09-29 | model | A named `concept` is a predicate over functions written as a list of requirements, applicable to any implementation; two implementations of the same concept are interchangeable.
r918 | 2026-09-29 | model | Un `concept` con nombre es un predicado sobre funciones escrito como lista de requisitos (una por línea); dos implementaciones del mismo concepto son intercambiables porque satisfacen las mismas leyes.
r919 | 2026-09-29 | model | A diferencia de C++, donde `requires` solo chequea sintaxis, acá una línea puede ser requisito de tipo (lo chequea el verificador sin correr) o requisito de valor (lo corre el test), con la misma sintaxis.
r920 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Asks whether type restriction exists, e.g. `N` restricted to `N<7`.
r921 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Pregunta si existe la restricción de tipos, por ejemplo `N` restringido a `N < 7`.
r922 | 2026-09-29 | model | Going from `{n:N|n<7}` to `N` is free (`fst`, forgetting the guarantee); going from `N` to the subset with a computed value requires checking it, a cast with content that verifies or fails.
r923 | 2026-09-29 | model | A subset type is already in the core as the pair of a value with evidence it satisfies the predicate; a restriction on an argument is a precondition, on the result a postcondition, both tested with the same machine as `requires`; it is `concept` applied to values instead of functions.
r924 | 2026-09-29 | model | Un tipo de subconjunto `{ n : N | n < 7 }` es azúcar del par de un valor con la evidencia de que cumple el predicado; una restricción en argumento es precondición, en resultado es postcondición, y el predicado puede mencionar argumentos anteriores (tipo dependiente). Pasar al supertipo es gratis (`fst`), pasar al subtipo requiere comprobar.
r925 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Pregunta por las formas en programación de decir que, por ejemplo, el tipo día es un entero `>0` y `<= 31`.
r926 | 2026-09-29 | model | Among range types across languages, L sits between construct-time checking and dependent types: the type is written as a refinement, checked on construction when the value comes from outside or a computation, and by evidence if some day statically; no way to have a `Dia` without the predicate being evaluated or proved.
r927 | 2026-09-29 | model | Ubica a L entre los tipos de rango de Ada/Pascal y los tipos dependientes: el tipo se escribe como refinement y la comprobación es al construir (test evalúa el predicado) o por evidencia si se quiere estática; no hay forma de tener un valor sin que el predicado haya sido evaluado o probado.
r928 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | The fundamental novelty of L: defining function types not only by argument and result types but also by postconditions, which are predicates of the arguments and the result; and normally a function is not defined alone but in groups (read/write).
r929 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Enuncia lo que para él es la novedad de L: definir tipos de funciones no solo con tipos de argumentos y resultados sino con postcondiciones (predicados de argumentos y resultado), y que normalmente no se define una función sola sino de a grupos (read/write).
r930 | 2026-09-29 | model | Each piece (Eiffel contracts, algebraic specs, type class laws, QuickCheck) exists somewhere; the novelty is that the group-with-laws as a type, with laws as data run by default and interchangeable implementations, is the normal form of defining anything, not a separate tool.
r931 | 2026-09-29 | model | Precision on the designer's point: the unit is not the function with postcondition but the group of functions with the laws that relate them; a single function's postcondition is the group of size one.
r932 | 2026-09-29 | model | Precisa que la unidad no es la función con postcondición sino el grupo de funciones con las leyes que las relacionan; la postcondición de una sola función es el caso de grupo de tamaño uno. Sitúa la novedad en que el grupo con sus leyes sea un tipo, una implementación un término, las leyes datos que se corren por defecto y se registran, con posibilidad de subir una ley de testeada a demostrada.
r933 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Dislikes introducing a new type `concept`; believes in recursive construction from functions; asks whether a concept can be a function.
r934 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | No le gusta introducir un tipo nuevo `concept` porque cree en la construcción recursiva a partir de funciones; pregunta si `concept` puede ser una función.
r935 | 2026-09-29 | model | A concept is an ordinary function returning a type (with proofs) or a list of laws (with tests); the test and proof versions are the same function with a different return type, and raising a law from tested to proved is mechanical.
r936 | 2026-09-29 | model | Confirma: un concepto es una función que devuelve una lista de leyes (versión tests) o un tipo (versión pruebas), sin primitiva nueva; `concept` y `requires` son azúcar. El pasaje de una versión a otra es mecánico, y un concepto puede tomar y combinar otros conceptos.
r937 | 2026-09-29 | model | Una ley es un tipo de entradas junto con un predicado sobre ellas (`Ley := (A : U) × (A -> Bool)`), de modo que el generador sabe qué muestrear y leyes de un mismo concepto pueden cuantificar sobre variables distintas.
r938 | 2026-09-29 | model | Writing the Almacen laws surfaces that `leer` for an absent key has nothing to return; the law forces deciding between `Maybe A`, a default value, or restricting `K` to present keys.
r939 | 2026-09-29 | model | Define `Almacen` con dos leyes, incluyendo que escribir en una clave no toca lo que había en otra; sin esa segunda ley un `write` que borra todo lo demás pasaría la primera. La ley obliga a decidir qué devuelve `leer` para una clave ausente.
r940 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Proposes that Almacen is a function taking a write and a read and returning `Maybe Almacen`, where Almacen hands back the verified versions of write and read.
r941 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Propone pensar `Almacen` como una función que toma un `write` y un `read` y devuelve `Maybe Almacen`, donde `Almacen` entrega las versiones verificadas de `write` y `read`.
r942 | 2026-09-29 | model | Checking a value is decided by evaluating it; checking a function cannot be, it must be sampled, so the function constructor carries `n` and depends on a generator; one check is exact, the other statistical, but both are the same construct operation.
r943 | 2026-09-29 | model | Confirms the designer's form, same as the validating constructor of `Dia`: the only way to make an Almacen is to pass the check; proposes three adjustments (return `Resultado` not `Maybe` to keep the counterexample; the certificate must state what "verified" means; it is a pattern).
r944 | 2026-09-29 | model | Para todo subconjunto hay un constructor automático que evalúa el predicado; sobre un número se decide evaluando, sobre una función hay que muestrear. L trata a ambos como la misma operación de construir, uno exacto, el otro estadístico.
r945 | 2026-09-29 | model | Confirma la forma como el constructor validante de `Dia`: la única manera de fabricar un `Almacen` es pasar por la comprobación, de modo que quien pide `Almacen S K A` no puede recibir un par sin verificar. Propone devolver `Resultado` en vez de `Maybe` para conservar el contraejemplo, y un `Certificado` que diga n casos, generador, rango, cuándo.
r946 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | The Almacen type confuses two things, the constructor of the almacen and the almacen itself; that construction pattern (not Almacen) is the fundamental concept of L.
r947 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Señala que el tipo `Almacen`, que confunde el constructor y lo construido, encarna el concepto fundamental de L: no `Almacen` sino el patrón de construcción.
r948 | 2026-09-29 | model | The pattern applied to itself is what the designer asked of L since May: the certificate accumulates by composition and `material` composed is the chained forgetting, with two testable laws.
r949 | 2026-09-29 | model | The primitive is the predicate; from a material `T` and predicate `P` three things derive mechanically (`Tipo P`, `construir P`, `material = fst`); they are not two things confused under a name but two uses of one thing; the pattern applies to itself (compiler is an instance) and the certified type of one stage is the material of the next.
r950 | 2026-09-29 | model | Nombra el patrón: dado material `T` y predicado `P`, salen mecánicamente el tipo certificado, el constructor (`construir`) y el olvido (`material = fst`); `Dia`, `Almacen`, `Resta` y el propio compilador (`Program` material, `check` constructor) son instancias. El patrón se aplica a sí mismo: el tipo certificado de una etapa es el material de la siguiente, con certificado acumulado por composición.
r951 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | L itself would be a series of verified types and functions with a set of theorems/tests; almost the L type.
r952 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Propone que L (el tipo) sea una serie de tipos y funciones verificadas con un conjunto de teoremas/tests.

r955 | 2026-09-29 | model | Each part has an owner (Eiffel for where the law goes, QuickCheck for running laws on generated inputs, dependent types for the group as a type); what the model does not know together is one pattern for values, functions and whole programs, the certificate as a first-class composable and forgettable value, and persisted runs as data of the language.
r956 | 2026-09-29 | model | After four searches, the top mechanism to steal: the certificate as a clause with hypotheses (from SPARK "Explicit Assumptions"), where each verification produces `H1 ∧ ... ∧ Hn → L` and a program's certificate is the closure of those clauses, giving `combinar` exact semantics and answering "if this falls, what falls".
r957 | 2026-09-29 | model | Of three named gaps, the place L can say something of its own: gradual verification has a two-point order (precise or hole); the three-point one, proved / tested with `n` cases / hole, is not formulated nor its guarantees proved.
r958 | 2026-09-29 | model | Sitúa el aporte: Eiffel da el lugar de la ley; QuickCheck la ejecución sobre entradas generadas; los tipos dependientes el grupo con leyes como tipo; proof-carrying code el certificado que viaja. Lo que no conoce junto es un mismo patrón para valores, funciones y programas, con certificado de primera clase componible y olvidable, y corridas persistidas, como forma normal de definir cualquier cosa.
r959 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Propone dar un nombre (L o ad-hoc) a esta estructura de verificación, que cree que es el núcleo de L y lo que lo haría útil e importante.
r960 | 2026-09-29 | model | Recomienda no llamar L a la estructura: L es el lenguaje, esto es lo que construye; un nombre separado evita el problema del constructor y lo construido y permite decir "L es el lenguaje cuyos programas son X". r963 | 2026-09-29 | model | Rechaza "Object" (propuesto por the designer en turno 69) porque evoca instancia de clase con estado y métodos, imagen que L no tiene (no hay mutación ni tiempo), y arrastra "object-oriented".
