r964 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Observa que ningún lenguaje tiene unidades (namespaces, units) que vengan con un conjunto de requisitos que deban cumplir, requisitos que pueden ser parametrizables (error máximo, escalabilidad, requerimientos).
r965 | 2026-09-29 | model | Confirma el diagnóstico y muestra que el requisito parametrizable es directo porque el predicado es una función con argumentos (`Numerica eps n_max`); el certificado pasa de sí/no a "cumple con estos parámetros", y una library que depende de otra hereda sus parámetros, lo que `combinar` debe verificar. La ley de escalabilidad usa `tiempo`, un hueco que depende de la máquina, que el certificado debe registrar.

r970 | 2026-09-29 | model | Reduce el patrón a una sola definición, `Subtype (T : U) (P : T -> Bool) := (x : T) × Certificado (P x)`, con `Dia`, `Resta`, `Almacen`, `Library` como casos, constructor único y `material = fst`; `concept` desaparece como noción aparte. r971 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Prefiere usar símbolos y no palabras para definir subtipos; los subtipos son predicados y en ese sentido tipos como cualquier otro, pero predicados que devuelven un mensaje de error (la condición en que fallan), y si esa condición es tipada el retorno es un tipo suma de éxito más tipos de error.
r972 | 2026-09-29 | model | Un predicado que devuelve `Resultado E` conserva el contraejemplo con su causa, y el tipo `E` lista los modos de fallo (como `Result<T,E>` de Rust); el error es un dato de un tipo inductivo, no un `Term`. Corrige: los subtipos no son predicados sino tipos; el predicado es la función, el subtipo el `Σ`, y `Subtype T P` hace el pasaje automático. Propone símbolos para formadores (`|`, `∀`, `::`) y palabras para constructores de datos.

r978 | 2026-09-29 | model | Propone `Ev f := (x : A) × (y : B) × (f x = y)` (el grafo de `f`), con azúcar `[f x]` que evalúa y guarda la terna; en versión 0 sin `=` se cae la última componente. `Fallo` se define como `Ev f` restringido, el mensaje de error es la impresión por defecto de un `Ev`, y la base de corridas es una lista de `Ev` con tiempo pegado. No pone azúcar a `B^A × A × B` sin restricción porque guardar una terna arbitraria no dice nada.

r980 | 2026-09-29 | model | A new symbol is only justified if it replaces a construction that would otherwise appear in every program; the set must not grow.

r987 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | `∀` is not ASCII, raised as a problem for the symbol choice.
r988 | 2026-09-29 | model | The canonical form of the language is ASCII; an editor may display `->` as `→` and `*` as `×` without changing the file, and what is run by hand and grepped is the ASCII.
r989 | 2026-09-29 | model | A quantified law needs no new symbol: the lambda's typed parameters are the "for all", a law is a function to `Bool`, and that is what makes the test runnable by hand.
r990 | 2026-09-29 | model | `+` for sum of types, since mathematics and type theory use it and type-sum never collides with number-sum; `|` is reserved for "such that".
r991 | 2026-09-29 | model | An n-ary variant is sugar: `Variant : List U -> U` defined by recursion over the list from binary `+`, so only binary `+` leans on `data`.
r992 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Asks about quote and evalstore as remaining operations.
r993 | 2026-09-29 | model | Quote gives a term's syntax as data without evaluating; evalstore is the pair of syntax and value, so with quote and application it is a pair, not a third operation; its only reason for a symbol is frequency.
r994 | 2026-09-29 | model | Quote must decide free variables: `'` keeps names (translation), `[| |]` substitutes bound variable values before storing (error message); the second is `subst` applied to the first.
r995 | 2026-09-29 | model | With juxtaposition syntax `' f x` is ambiguous, so quote needs delimiters; prefix plus braces `'{ }` and unquote `${ }` (Scala 3), no prefix without a closer.
r996 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Proposes the type of a predicate of A as `A -> Maybe ((A -> bool) * A * bool)` and asks how predicates concatenate.
r997 | 2026-09-29 | model | Two concatenations: conjunction `P & Q` (both pass, stop at first by default), and refinement `P |> Q` where `Q` is defined over the subtype `A | P` and order is not optional; nested subtypes flatten.
r998 | 2026-09-29 | model | Fixes the predicate type: the `Bool` of the triple is always false and `Maybe` is reversed; use `Predicate A := A -> Result (Failure A)` with `Failure` the failed law and its argument.
r999 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Asks how `match` is defined and what it is for.

r1001 | 2026-09-29 | model | `match` is the only way to open a `data` value; it is not primitive but sugar of the recursor that `data` gives, the translation is mechanical, and that is what guarantees termination and totality.
r1002 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Proposes drafting L's BNF.
r1003 | 2026-09-29 | model | Since `Resta` is a subtype, `resta : Resta := sub` forces the verifier to run the cases at compile time; alternative is leaving it as a hole until an explicit `test`.
r1004 | 2026-09-29 | model | The core fits in ten lines of EBNF with three reserved words (`data`, `match`, `extern`); each sugar rule carries its core translation and may add nothing to `Term`.
r1005 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Doubts using `|` to mark where predicates begin, wants something like `requires`.
r1006 | 2026-09-29 | model | `|` has three jobs and is the worst candidate for a construction one wants to recognize by eye; proposes `where` for the named-predicate subtype and dropping `|` from clauses in favor of repeating the name.
r1007 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | `muestreado 1000` makes no sense in the type definition and proposes naming the candidate variable and applying the laws to it explicitly.

r1009 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Asks what `r` is and how one knows it, and raises that distributivity couples several functions.
r1010 | 2026-09-29 | model | Coupled functions mean the material is a product; the pattern `(add, mul)` unpacks it and names each component, so laws like distributivity are written over named components.
r1011 | 2026-09-29 | model | Settle on set-builder notation `{ patrón : material | predicado }` as the single subtype form; the variable's type is written beside it, the pattern names components, and `|` keeps a single meaning inside braces.
r1012 | 2026-09-29 | model | The form that shows what matters puts the structure's named components with their types above the `|` and the laws below, exactly as a ring is written in an algebra book; `forget` names the projection from the certificate, language names in English.
r1013 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | If set-builder notation is so nice and familiar, why is it not used in any language, or is it?
r1014 | 2026-09-29 | model | Set-builder subtype exists in Coq, Lean, F*, Z; mainstream languages omit it because deciding the predicate needs a solver, a proof, or a runtime check; L's difference is a fourth way, testing with `n` cases and recording it in the certificate, which lets `p` be any function to `Bool`, including laws over functions.
r1015 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Had not realized Python's list comprehensions are set-builder notation.
r1016 | 2026-09-29 | model | A comprehension filters values that already exist; `{ x : T | p x }` as a type says which values are acceptable and one must fabricate one and pass the check; the `test` crosses exactly this, listing candidates for `N` but generating law inputs for `N -> N -> N`.
r1017 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Feels odd wanting a modern HoTT language yet ending up with primary-school set notation, but what is effective is effective.
r1018 | 2026-09-29 | model | The set-builder notation denotes a `Σ`, a fundamental construction of type theory and HoTT, so primary school and HoTT coincide here; what was left from HoTT is the proof apparatus, reserved as a certificate level "demostrado".
r1019 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Category theory tries to bring everything to sets, so using sets for everything is not contradictory.
r1020 | 2026-09-29 | model | Corrects the direction (categories generalize sets); what defines the subtype categorically is the mono `forget`, the arrow, not the elements, which is what holds when `T` is a function type and the same pattern applies to `N`, `N -> N -> N` and `Program`.
r1021 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Asks what `Associative` is, whether it should be a type too, and whether he should write `add in Associative`.
r1022 | 2026-09-29 | model | `Associative` is both a predicate over `A -> A -> A` and the subtype `{ f | Associative A f }`, same name two uses; `add in Associative` is sugar for the applied predicate since `x in { y : T | p y }` is `p x`, not adopted; and a third reading puts the law in the component's type.
r1023 | 2026-09-29 | model | Laws take the operation first and the quantified variables after, so `Associative A f` is a partially applied predicate over which the `test` generates `x y z`.
r1024 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | Asks for the state of the language's primitive elements (types, functions, elements).
r1025 | 2026-09-29 | model | Lays out the language in three layers: a core (seven term forms plus `data`, definition, and reduction), a base library defined in L, and sugar with mechanical translation; no `Bool`, numbers, `=`, `Σ` or `Subtype` in the core.
r1026 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | A human thinks in primitive elements (like objects and arrows in category theory, or statements, expressions, variables, types, functions in C++) and builds up; starting with something complex like `Term` is confusing; wants a conceptual starting point to think in L.
r1027 | 2026-09-29 | model | Giving a name to a value is the only act of the language; a program is a set of definitions whose order does not matter, dependencies between names give the graph.
r1028 | 2026-09-29 | model | The whole language reduces to ten words in the order for thinking (bottom-up, not starting from `Term`): tipo, valor, función, definición, hueco; función dependiente, dato, subtipo; aplicar, reducir.
r1029 | 2026-09-29 | model | Of the ten elements, eight are standard type theory and two are L's own: the hole as a first-class concept and the subtype whose predicate is checked by test and certified.
r1030 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | A type can be thought of in three ways (as definition/typing, as constructor, as proposition); the information is the same, the program uses it for three different purposes.
r1031 | 2026-09-29 | model | Base types have two uses (typing, constructing); the third use (asking as a predicate) only exists for subtypes, and is the one L adds; typing and constructing are judgements of the verifier, asking is computation that leaves a certificate.
r1032 | 2026-09-29 | model | The distinction between base type and predicate is structural (every value has a representation), but where the base ends and predicates begin is a design decision that decides what is computation and what is data.
r1033 | 2026-09-29 | the designer | It is not true that integers are inside the reals; reals are more complex objects. You can inject the integers into the reals but that does not make them reals.
r1034 | 2026-09-29 | model | Subtype `{x:T|p x}` is for restrictions within one representation; when two types have different representations their relation is always a certified function with laws, never a subtype, and there is no implicit coercion.
r1035 | 2026-09-30 | model | `extern` is a hole with a label saying who fills it; L knows only the type and does not reduce it, it is the border between what runs by hand and what does not, and the first candidate to carry laws. It could even be an ordinary library function rather than a keyword.
r1036 | 2026-09-30 | the designer | Semantically a day is a day and has nothing to do with integers; the difference between natural types and predicative types looks like an implementation detail.
r1037 | 2026-09-30 | model | Rule for which construction: `data` when the thing has its own form and operations (N, List, Result, Term), subtype when it is a restriction of something that already has operations (Dia, Subtraction, Ring); both are types of equal status, one carries a certificate and the other does not.
r1038 | 2026-09-30 | model | `d : Dia` and `d : {n:N | esDia n}` are the same by definition; `Dia` is a name for the subtype and the name is the interface, nobody writes the long form.
r1039 | 2026-09-30 | model | The three uses: `d : Dia` is the judgement (returns nothing), `Dia m` is the question (returns `Result`), `Dia(34)` is construction (returns `Result Dia` with the day inside); constructing is asking and packaging, and `forget d` recovers the base value.
r1040 | 2026-09-30 | the designer | `Maybe_error<T>` (a variant of T and string) was useful in macro_dr, but errors should be more explicit; the worry is the resulting complex/dynamic types, asking whether those are dependent types.
r1041 | 2026-09-30 | model | The error sum `E1 + E2` is an ordinary type computed by the verifier from the types of `f` and `g`, not dynamic nor dependent; three ways to manage error growth (open single type, exact sum, explicit forgetting at borders), with exact sum inside and forgetting at borders.
r1042 | 2026-09-30 | model | Distinguish the type of possible error (computed before running, linear in the chain, small) from the error that occurred (computed at run time, a datum with variable structure given by List/Variant/tree).
r1043 | 2026-09-30 | the designer | Iteration whose length comes from a datum is not rare but the most common case; it appears with MCMC and the like.
r1044 | 2026-09-30 | model | Iteration needs three forms of propagation to design: cut at first fail (`Result`), accumulate partial fails and continue, and decorate the value with an accumulating adornment (acceptances, log-likelihood, time) where the forget functor decides what is kept; a rejected step is a diagnostic, not an error.
r1045 | 2026-09-30 | the designer | Having lived the combinatorial nightmare of C++ template types, types should be semantic (defined by a number or label keeping a common structure); an error is ultimately a predicate that fails, representable as a recursively growing json-like structure.
r1046 | 2026-09-30 | model | A single recursive error type `Failure`, a tree with label, argument and a list of sub-failures, where the labels are not strings but quotes of program terms; `Result` collapses to `ok A + fail Failure` without `E`, and `Failure` and the success certificate are the same structure, probably a single `Report` with a verdict per node.
r1047 | 2026-09-30 | the designer | Asks whether here they are using code-as-data in their favor.
r1048 | 2026-09-30 | model | Code as data is the midpoint between two nightmares (string gives a log, a type per law gives the template explosion); `what` as a term has the structure of a type and the uniformity of a datum, making failures reproducible, navigable, comparable and forgettable with control. This is why `Term` is in the base library, not a metaprogramming module.
r1049 | 2026-09-30 | model | The three ingredients of `Failure` exist separately elsewhere (tree of causes in anyhow/Go, reproducible counterexample in Hypothesis/QuickCheck, quoted form in Lisp/Lean) but their combination into one object that is also the success certificate requires a language where code is data from the start.

r1051 | 2026-09-30 | model | `Maybe_error` is `Failure` with the tree flattened to a string (maximal forgetting); two decisions now make the later change mechanical: `Result A` and `fail` carry a named type `Failure` (today `:= String`), and the string is always built with one `report what arg` function.
r1052 | 2026-09-30 | the designer | Worry that functions would have to have self-knowledge, which is not trivial.
r1053 | 2026-09-30 | model | Functions need no self-knowledge; the law is an ordinary function to `Bool` and the label is put by whoever runs it. `test` is not an ordinary L function but an operation over `Program` (like `check` and `eval`) that knows the whole program, reads a definition's laws, runs, and returns the `Program` with the certificate added.
r1054 | 2026-09-30 | model | A single `Result A := ok A + fail Failure` serves both constructor and verifier uses; the question is construction with the value discarded, composition is `and_then`/`>>=`, and `test` returns `Result Program` with the certified program inside.
r1055 | 2026-09-30 | model | Laws live in the type (option A), with `requires` under a definition as sugar for an anonymous subtype; `Def` stays with three fields and `test` always reads laws from the type. Consequence: laws live in the DAG like any definition, so changing a law invalidates dependents' certificates via the graph.
r1056 | 2026-09-30 | model | `Ring` shows what `Subtraction` does not: laws shared across structures (`Associative` defined once), a law crossing components (`Distributive add mul` belonging to the pair not to either operation), and subtype-over-subtype inheritance (`Field` includes `Ring`'s certificate); the operation-first convention lets `test` know which variables to generate and which come from the structure.

r1191 | 2026-10-02 | model | The likelihood is an object reporting its Fisher information H; the diagnostic compares reported H against delivered J over true-process data, yielding the displacement b = H⁻¹E[S] and distortion C = H^(−1/2) J H^(−1/2) as measured quantities; declaring the likelihood is the sampler's density is declaring b = 0 and C = I within a sampling floor; the profiled likelihood is a member with m = K/(K−2), not a failure.

r1276 | 2026-10-03 | model | Gives a theoretical correlate to the designer's two ways: the C++ closed model tightly couples representation to operations; the typeclass/theory-of-models model is an open contract (declares types, operations, axioms) that a concrete implementation satisfies as a model.
r1277 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | A type can be declared (universal) before it is defined; while only declared it is like a pure class that cannot be instantiated or used, e.g. you cannot declare a variable is a Table until Table is defined with its construction and functions.
r1278 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | Declaring that something is of a type should already guarantee a series of methods/properties; building a Table gives the guarantee you can use all its methods, which he recognizes is rediscovering object-oriented programming.
r1279 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | An operation whose invariants are defined in terms of other operations requires those operations to already be defined; otherwise it is invalid.
r1280 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | He wants an effective L that does something (e.g. declare in laws that one function is the inverse of another) without defining the invariants of every element, testing the minimum first and advancing from there.
r1281 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | A loose free syntax where you declare various invariants freely and then say which ones you want to ensure; he thinks this is the logic current L (without its Claude) follows, and it is expressive and fine for writing tests independently of each other.
r1282 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | Many functions and types belong together as a single mathematical object (group, monoid); you cannot define one part alone, the object is defined whole.
r1283 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | Operations live in an ideal error-free world and are approximated in reality (reals by floating point); laws hold not exactly but with a precision guarantee, and the tradeoff between guarantee and reachable values becomes an optimization problem.
r1284 | 2026-10-03 | the designer | An invariant like "sum is the inverse of subtraction" would otherwise be defined twice, once per function; instead the invariant belongs to the pair.
r1285 | 2026-10-03 | model | The difficulty is one of type semantics, not syntax; Claude invents ad-hoc syntax or falls into traditional property-based testing because it lacks the formal semantics anchored.
r1286 | 2026-10-03 | model | The circularity resolves because laws are equations over the joint signature, not recursive operational definitions; a theory packages the cyclic graph of relations into one semantic unit where no function owns another.

r2000 | 2026-10-05 | the designer | A principle of simplicity makes a check return a Bool, but a monad applied over the check can make it return a checked type; both functions are valid and the context decides which to use.
r2001 | 2026-10-05 | model | The two presentations are the characteristic function and the partial inverse of inclusion; each derives from the other with one combinator (`refine p x = if p x then just x else nothing`), so the check is written once as Bool and the refined version is derived, provided the checker knows that in the `then` branch `p x` holds. The context that decides is whether the evidence must survive outside the branch; a branch condition different from the predicate turns the refinement into an obligation that is itself a certified law.

r2098 | 2026-10-05 | model | Un `match` sobre tipos rompería la parametricidad en la que se apoya G5 (probar una ley genérica en una instancia), porque probar una instancia vale solo si la función no puede distinguir tipos.
r2099 | 2026-10-05 | model | El universo U tiene introducción (todo tipo es valor de U) pero su columna de eliminación está vacía: una función puede recibir y usar un `T : U` pero no hacer `match` sobre su forma.
r2100 | 2026-10-05 | model | Un universo de códigos dentro del lenguaje: un `data Code` describe las formas de tipo generables, `El : Code -> U` las decodifica, y los funtores pasan a ser funciones de L `sampled : (c : Code) -> Metric -> Gen (El c)`; hace literales los dos principios (generadores en L, solo de tipos definidos) sin reflexión ni `match` sobre U.
r2101 | 2026-10-05 | model | In the kernel (04 §2) the universe `U` has introduction (every type) but an empty elimination column: a function can receive and use a `T : U` but cannot ask a type what shape it has.
r2102 | 2026-10-05 | model | A `match` over types would break the parametricity on which G5 rests, since proving a generic law on one instance is valid only if the function cannot distinguish types.
r2103 | 2026-10-05 | model | A `data Code` describes the type shapes we can generate, `El : Code -> U` decodes each, and the functors become ordinary L functions `sampled : (c : Code) -> Metric -> Gen (El c)`; this makes both of the designer's principles literal and needs no `match` over U.
r2104 | 2026-10-06 | model | Los tipos recursivos se describen con dos constructores más (`var` y `mu`) como punto fijo `μX. F(X)`; `Code` no admite `var` a la izquierda de una flecha, así que todo punto fijo que describe cumple positividad estricta y es legítimo.
r2105 | 2026-10-06 | model | Implementar el universo de códigos por etapas: primero sin `mu` (números con límites, productos, sumas, vectores, refinamientos), con `List` por un código propio y `mu` con muestreo de Boltzmann cuando aparezca un `data` recursivo que haga falta generar.
r2106 | 2026-10-06 | model | Generar un valor de `μF` necesita controlar la terminación; para un punto fijo el costo es el tamaño y las cuentas por tamaño salen del código sin mirar ningún valor (Boltzmann, Feat), bajo la misma regla de costos acumulados que los números y los censos.
r2107 | 2026-10-06 | model | `Code` y `El` se definen a la vez, cada una usando a la otra (inducción-recursión, Dybjer 2000); en el vocabulario de L ese par es un ciclo, o sea un objeto (05 §3).
r2108 | 2026-10-06 | model | `Code` and `El` are defined mutually (induction-recursion, Dybjer 2000); in L's vocabulary that pair is a cycle, i.e. an object (05 §3), the way L already understands mutual definitions.
r2109 | 2026-10-06 | model | Recursive types need two more constructors, `var` and `mu : Code -> Code`, so a code describes a fixpoint `μX. F(X)`; strict positivity holds for free because `Code` has no arrow with `var` on the left, and generating a fixpoint uses size or Boltzmann/Feat cost-weighting (the designer's cumsum of costs).
r2110 | 2026-10-06 | model | Recommends the codes universe, done in stages: first codes without `mu` (numbers with bounds, products, sums, vectors, refinements), `List` with its own code for now, `mu` with Boltzmann sampling when a recursive `data` needs generating; functions over infinite domains go by census (G9).
r2111 | 2026-10-06 | model | In Russell, `U` is open (each program adds its types) so a `match` on `U` can't be exhaustive and the kernel has no elimination for `U`; in Tarski, `U` can be an ordinary inductive `data` of codes, closed, and then `match` by induction on codes is available.
r2112 | 2026-10-06 | model | The code universe asks of L three things: mutual definition of `Code` and `El` (induction-recursion, a cycle/object per 05), large elimination (`El` returning a type by `match`), and that `match` refine the expected type in each branch, which is R11's dependent pattern matching already planned.
r2113 | 2026-10-06 | model | A closed Tarski universe describes only types that have a code; what lacks a code (a declared-but-undefined type, a type arrived as a parameter) can't be inspected, which is exactly what is wanted.
r2114 | 2026-10-06 | model | Proposal for L: keep Russell's `U` for everything, and add a small closed Tarski universe as a library for what must be treated generically (generators, structural equality, scope of a certificate); a code for `U` itself is excluded to avoid Girard's paradox through `El`.
r2115 | 2026-10-06 | model | Two ways to answer what an element of a universe `U` is: Russell, where an element of `U` is itself a type used directly; Tarski, where an element is a code (a name) and a decoding function `El` carries each code to the type it names.
r2116 | 2026-10-06 | model | Having both universes makes a function's signature carry what it can do: `(A : U) -> …` is parametric and can't look at `A` (free theorems hold); `(c : Code) -> …` may inspect the shape of `c`. A typecase on `U` breaks parametricity silently; the code universe makes it explicit.
r2117 | 2026-10-06 | the designer | the designer asks how `El` is read.
r2118 | 2026-10-06 | model | `El` abbreviates *elements*: `El c` is "the elements of `c`", the type whose values are the elements of what the code `c` names; the name is a convention and could be `typeOf`, `decode` or `Meaning`.
r2119 | 2026-10-06 | the designer | the designer asks why `El` was used and not `typeOf`, whether it is more precise.

r2125 | 2026-10-06 | model | A language is elegant when its constructions follow from one or two ideas and its interpreter in the same language fits a page; mosaic when each construction was added for a need and the spec is a list of interactions.
r2126 | 2026-10-06 | model | L's specification is deliberately on the elegant side (04 one canonical core, each surface a translation), but the interpreter already has mosaic pieces; the code universe is Steele's move, generators entering as library definitions rather than new interpreter constructions.
r2127 | 2026-10-06 | model | Recent popular languages are not better founded; theory arrives late and piecemeal. What changed is that a language's metatheory can now be machine-verified, so an old language can end up better founded than a new one. L is on the side born from a theory.
r2128 | 2026-10-06 | model | Because L certifies refinement types by sampling rather than SMT, what in other languages is the power of the prover is here the quality of the sample.

r2130 | 2026-10-06 | the designer | the designer asks whether L can fill an existing need and let one express things not expressible in other languages.
r2131 | 2026-10-06 | model | The combination the model claims no language joins: laws in the types, checked by sampling with named generators and statistical levels, evidence tied by hash to code and interpreter, and the result carrying which laws it rests on.
r2132 | 2026-10-06 | model | The model's opinion that L should be a language to state and certify the claims of a computation done in Python or R, not a language to program in, as a smaller and more reachable target.
r2133 | 2026-10-06 | model | L adds no new expressiveness over Lean or Agda; its novelty is the evidence regime, making numerical and statistical claims verifiable at a scientist's cost. The gap is between proof and nothing.
r2134 | 2026-10-06 | the designer | the designer's founding idea: types as propositions extended to functions (which are types), where a function's type is its postconditions and invariants, and belonging to a type verifies a set of propositions "a full".
r2135 | 2026-10-06 | model | The founding idea is Martin-Löf 1979 and is the field's central one; what distinguishes L is carrying it "a full" to propositions that cannot be proved. The idea mixes two readings of type-as-proposition (Curry-Howard witness vs refinement/contract), and L has both.
r2136 | 2026-10-06 | model | By Rice's theorem "a full" cannot be decided automatically, so for the claim to be honest the grade of verification (proved, exhaustive, census, sampled with these generators and level) must be part of what belonging to a type affirms.
r2137 | 2026-10-06 | the designer | the designer's idea of joining testing and types into one thing, and adding self-optimization: from a set of implementations {f} of F, choose the fastest for your particular case, and store that choice.
r2138 | 2026-10-06 | model | An order that cannot be skipped: optimizing over weak certificates is worse than not optimizing, because it changes results silently over an unverified equivalence; generators and reach first, selection after.
r2139 | 2026-10-06 | model | Four consequences of self-optimization: replacement is safe only as far as F's laws say and the chosen implementation is part of each result's provenance; selection only among implementations whose certificate covers the case (reach of C6); "faster" is a statistical verdict with level stored per machine and case class; rewriting via equality saturation is legitimate only within the rounding-law tolerance.
r2140 | 2026-10-06 | model | Type, certification and measurement are one idea from three sides: the type says what is asked, certification says which implementations belong and over what input range, measurement says which is fastest; what makes selection safe is the certification, and it is the algorithm selection problem (Rice 1976).
r2141 | 2026-10-06 | model | Without a written `docs/objectives.md`, a model deciding on its own within its margin has nothing to measure its decisions against; P4 and P12 are measured against those objectives.
r2142 | 2026-10-06 | model | Restating the L draft, the model names a tension between L's O1 (be useful now, the application first) and laying the foundations first; the cost falls on the application, whose `.lut` layer must be rewritten on every kernel change.

r2284 | 2026-10-08 | the designer | How to represent evaluation steps in L: the states of memory (2; 2+; 2+2; 4), possibly with pointers to a partially applied function, with syntax/type errors caught along the way (arcsin of a string, a value out of range).
r2285 | 2026-10-08 | the designer | Cómo representás la evaluación de una expresión en L. Una forma consume los símbolos con poca memoria (un "más" ya es una función que suma 2 a lo siguiente); otra guarda los tres símbolos y después ejecuta. Los pasos serían el estado de la memoria (un 2, después 2+, después 2+2, después 4), y eso cree que es universal. Dos tipos de cosas: el símbolo + con su 2, o ya una función + rellena del 2 (aplicación parcial), o un puntero a F(A,B).
